CursorCorolla Point · 22%
Risk Top weight and a cold McDonald (0-28 J14) in a 14-runner sprint where nothing dominates.
live pace map with Bethell dual entryground speed within Goodnon-public stable tactics between Corolla Point and Fluorescence
Corolla Point is the right side of this handicap on merit, not on price alone: OR 97, a clean placing profile (2-3-3), and proven CD (1-1) at a track where his high draw (11) fits the 39% high-band bias. The Newcastle reference run is strong — beaten only 0.3L in this grade with several rivals behind him today — and he maps as the form standard. I do not read 3.8 as proof; it still makes him the likeliest winner, but in a 14-runner Class 2 sprint that is closer to ~22% than the model’s 47%.

The main danger is the race compressing late rather than CP failing outright: Vantheman (Hamilton 5f winner, draw 9, 0.2L at Newcastle) and Canon’s House (draw 12, CD 1-1, same Newcastle form line) are both positioned to exploit the draw bias if McDonald misses the break from 11 under top weight. Arklow Lad and Jordan Electrics bring fresh winning spark, but ground (Arklow G 0-3) and trip/class profile (Jordan’s 6f Ayr win) leave them behind the top two on this specific test.

Risk Bintknight is only two runs old and the 2.5 quote looks short against Percival's sharper Sandown platform.
pace map / run-style match-uppost-Sandown fitness for Percival and Londonerwhether Starlight Time's AW Listed win transfers to 10f GTF turf
Bintknight leads on true chance (~29%): she has the clearest course-and-distance proof (Lingfield 10f Good winner), the lowest OR (77) in a tight handicap, a useful Ascot defeat only 0.1L in stronger company, and Lewis Edmunds is riding well. Draw 3 is neutral-to-fair on the Lingfield 10f map (low 39%, high 34%). That said, two career starts and 55% rtf leave stamina/fitness and mark-catchup questions, and the market’s 40% implied win rate is ahead of what the form justifies.

The main danger to that ranking is Percival (~24%): third at Sandown over 10f on good-to-firm, beaten just 0.2L off a 29.0 SP, reads like the most in-form improver and outpointed Londoner (same race, 1.8L further back) on that key recent test. Londoner gets draw 1 but 0-6 on good going is a serious GTF negative; Starlight Time brings Roger Varian upside but is unproven at this trip/going; Epictetus is a Lingfield course runner but too inconsistent to trust. Nothing here offers enough edge at the prices — hence no bet, with Bintknight still the likeliest winner on balance.

pace map / early speed rolessectional or timefigure comparison vs First Time's Doncaster runwhether 9st 9lb is a burden on Good To Firm here
Haaderr is the most convincing 7f profile in the field: top weight off OR 85 with proven ability at the trip (close seconds at Haydock and Thirsk on good ground, win at Southwell over 7f), and draw six sits in Doncaster's strongest seven-furlong bucket. Oisin Orr and Edward Bethell are the sharpest connection pairing on the card, and he maps as the horse most likely to run to a career-best figure rather than merely exploiting a soft mark — which matters when the market has him level with First Time.

The main pushback is form direction, not ability: reading right, his latest is a fourth, and the model's 58% reads like enthusiasm in a six-runner handicap where your settled ≥0.40 claims have landed only 41%. First Time is the clear danger — third over this course and trip two weeks ago, three pounds better off, and proven around Doncaster — but Barry McHugh's cold J14 ledger and a less convincing high-draw edge for Haaderr's main rival keep Haaderr narrowly on top at roughly 30% true win chance versus ~26% for First Time.

early pace/lead mapin-race fitness or veterinary flagsfine ground within Good To Firm
Moonhall Lass is the clearest win angle on form, not price: she won last time over this trip on Good to Firm in Class 6 at Leicester, and her profile fits today better than most — 2-4 on good ground and 1-6 at 7f, with James Owen’s yard in strong form. Get Up Everybody is consistent and well treated by the market, but he is still 0-1 at 7f with no going wins and cold Nolan/Midgley numbers; Emerald Army maps well on draw and trip but keeps finding one too good.

The main danger is the handicap itself: Moonhall Lass carries 9-12 off OR 60 from draw 6 while several rivals (Emerald Army, Get Up Everybody, Ryefield) enjoy the high-draw edge at Doncaster; if the race sets up for hold-up types or a low-draw front-runner, her win chance drops materially despite the Leicester form line.

CursorEgoli · 13%
Risk Class-3-to-Class-2 rise may expose him against stronger rivals despite the Doncaster win.
exact pace map with Marengo Storm/Egoli both p1live ground variant vs listed good to firmRichard Hughes yard preference between Marengo Storm and Startled
Egoli looks the most fairly treated runner on trip, going, and handicap terms. He won over this distance on good to firm 14 days ago at Doncaster in a nine-runner field, carries a workable 8-7 from OR 89 rather than ceiling weight, and his 2-4 going record is the strongest in the field. Draw five sits in the favoured lower third at Goodwood (~7f low-draw bias ~40%), and a front-running profile should suit if he can dictate without a hard duel against Marengo Storm from one.

The main doubt is class: that Doncaster win was Class 3 and this is a deep Class 2 with several horses already proven at this level on this course—notably Startled and Ironwill over seven furlongs here, plus Wild Desert dropping from Class 1 with top speed figures and a hot Appleby/Buick combination. I make Egoli only a ~13% chance in a wide 13-runner handicap, well below the model’s 50% on Wild Desert; market favouritism for We’re Goosers looks fragile after the Ascot collapse, and Proud Nation’s York form is undermined by stall 13 in the weakest draw band.

CursorSanafi Zabeel · 18%

Likely pace shape with multiple prominent typesGood vs Good-Firm from his May Hamilton winMwafaq top-weight effect under a 5lb-claiming jockey
**Sanafi Zabeel** looks the most likely winner on profile rather than price. He has already won at Hamilton over 8f on near-identical ground from a handy draw, drops back to his best trip after a stamina stretch-out flop, and pairs the card’s strongest trainer (Jim Goldie) with Paul Mulrennan, who has been effective from low draws in recent post-mortems. At OR 65 carrying 9-6 he is sensibly handicapped in a field where several rivals are burdened with higher marks, and draw 3 fits a Hamilton 8f map that only mildly favors high stalls.

The main risk is that his case rests heavily on one sharp Hamilton success and a thin four-run record; the Ayr defeat only two weeks ago leaves fitness and temperament slightly unconfirmed at this level. **Starliner** is the closest danger on recent 8f form and strong course-and-distance credentials (1-7 CD, fresh second at Ayr), while **Mwafaq** must be respected as the latest course winner but looks a short-priced top-weight under a claimer with no recent jockey wins — context, not proof.

Distinct Spirit is the most convincing form horse: a decisive Good-ground 8f win four days ago at Ffos Las, preceded by close thirds at Yarmouth (GF) and Newbury, with an 88% recent-trainer figure and a middle draw that fits Lingfield’s ~8f bias far better than the low stalls. Luke Catton’s competent recent strike rate adds a small jockey edge; I am not deferring to the OR 79 or his 1.3 SP last time as proof alone, but the run profile — travelling well and finishing — suits today’s trip and going better than anyone else in the field.

The main danger is Allegrino: Andrew Balding’s hot yard, a fresh Yarmouth GF win, and perfect micro-samples on going (1-1) and distance (2-2) are real positives at a useful handicap mark, and Bold Suitor’s Lingfield AW course knowledge is stale after 52 days with zero recent-trainer form. Allegrino’s draw 1 is the serious flaw here (low stalls win only ~18% at this trip), and Wetsand’s high draw plus strong going record (2-4) only partly offsets miserable recent figures — which keeps Distinct Spirit narrowly on top at roughly 28% true win chance in a genuinely open five-runner handicap.

Risk Top weight (9-11) in a deep 16-runner handicap despite a 0-7 good-ground record away from this C&D win.
early pace/map with several possible leadersAsuka's two-start ceiling at Class 3 7fexact Good-ground speed bias today
Yorkshire is the runner I'd side with, but not at a 44% win rate. He is the top official handicapper (OR 92), has the ideal high draw (16) in a race where high stalls have taken nearly half of winners, and is a proven course-and-distance winner with a neck-second at Doncaster over 7f in similar grade/class company two starts back. That recent run reads stronger than the favourite's two-race profile and better than most of the field's latest efforts.

The biggest danger is that this is still a wide-open Class 3 sprint: Yorkshire carries the highest weight, his overall good-ground tally is poor on paper (0-7), and a progressive, well-weighted rival from a low draw — chiefly Asuka, who looks short at 2.8 — could improve past him even if Yorkshire runs his race. Angel Hunter is my next best: consistent placed form at York over 7f, Richard Hannon's yard in strong shape, and draw 13 fits the bias. I would not treat the market favourite as proof of superiority here.

Spell Master is the most grounded angle in a messy 18-runner handicap: 1-1 at Goodwood, a tidy third at Newmarket (July) beaten only 0.3L on good ground, and a fair mid-low draw in a race where low is only slightly disadvantaged (~27% vs ~36% mid/high). OR 89 off top weight is workable, and he profiles as a horse who handles this track’s camber and undulations better than several shorter-priced rivals who are mainly 8f specialists or big-field Ascot victims.

The main reservation is trip — his recent runs are 7f–8f and today’s 9f asks a new question against Ammes, Boyfriend, El Matador and Thunder Wonder, who have clearer stamina evidence. Crest Of Fire is the likeliest usurper on pure form (Ascot second, 0.1L, top OR 94), but I rate him below market at 6.0 given 0-4 on good to firm, no course/distance proof, and a wide draw; Thunder Wonder (9f form, kind mark OR 83, Ghiani) is the best each-way counter-case if the race truly stretches the 8f types.

Lord Abama looks the most logically placed runner: 3 wins from 9 at Thirsk over 6f (CD 3-9), including a 0.3L near-miss here in April, and he now races off OR 56 — roughly 9lb below the top weights — from draw 4 in the favoured mid band (30% of winners) while low stalls are only 23%. Billy Garritty’s recent strike rate helps, and the handicap gives him a realistic path without needing to match Northcliff or Great on raw ratings alone.

The main danger is Kings Merchant, who is also a proven Thirsk six-furlong horse (CD 2-5, 40% strike rate) from draw 5 with a competitive mark; he maps as the clearest alternative if Lord Abama’s long layoff and last-two poor runs (7/11 Redcar, AW defeat) prove the course edge has faded. Northcliff’s market support (5.9→3.7) overstates him for me: draw 2 is the wrong side of a strong high-draw bias, his recent line (081460) is patchy, and he is 0-0 at Thirsk despite top weight and Silvestre De Sousa.

Pisanello is the most plausible winner on evidence form can give you: back-to-back C6 wins inside three days, including 9f on good ground, at the top mark he has earned. That beats Braes Of Doune’s patchier line (6th at Ayr, only 0-3 over 9f) and Hostelry’s excellent Hamilton record (2-5 course-and-distance, draw 8 in the slightly favoured high third). Draw 1 is neutral-to-fine here; the low-draw edge is modest (35% over six years), so I am not upgrading him mainly on stall.

I would not stake Braes at a 48% true win rate in a nine-runner 0-55 — calibration on high claims is closer to two in five winners, and nothing here is that dominant. Hostelry is the main danger: proven on this track, well drawn, consistently beaten only small margins, and more realistically in the low twenties than the market’s ~17% at 6.0. Defence Missile is a loose each-way speck on draw 2 and a hot Perratt yard, but an AW Newcastle win with 0-7 on good turf keeps him behind the front three for win purposes.

Jm Jhingree is my marginal top pick because he has the clearest recent proof on today's exact test: he won over 5f at Hamilton on 25 June (Good to Firm, class 6) off this same draw band, and his 2-4 course-and-distance record is the most directly relevant credential in the field. Draw eight fits a Hamilton 5f card where high stalls have won 39% of races over six years; he carries a workable 9-0 from OR 66, and Greg Fairley's 1-3 J14 record adds a small positive. I rate him around 15% true — well below Hundred Caps's market (~21%) — because top weight without a good-ground win profile and a thin 20% rtf figure make the favorite an easy horse to oppose on ability rather than price.

The main danger is that this is a fragmented handicap where several rivals bring Hamilton form without needing a short price: Thunderstorm Katie (second best) has been beaten only a neck and two lengths in two recent Hamilton runs and is well treated on 8-9, though draw two fights the course bias; Iris Dancer and Stirrup Cup get the high draws but both look more natural at six furlongs; Never Dark's 1-9 Hamilton CD record keeps him live if reviving. None of that makes Jhingree a strong betting…

He is 3-3 at Goodwood and 3-3 course-and-distance, including a decisive win over this trip here in May; that matters more than the co-favourite tag on Sondad because Goodwood six-furlong draw bias is essentially flat (mid/high 77% of winners). Sondad is a very close second: the Curragh win two weeks ago is frank, he has the better good-ground record (2-5 vs 0-3), and his Ascot defeat was similarly tight, but he lacks this specific course proof and faces a slightly trickier passage from draw 21 in a full gate.

The biggest risk is that this is a 28-runner sprint handicap where several rivals bring comparable form from that same Ascot reference — especially Completely Random (0.2L second, ideal mid draw, Ryan Moore) and the high-mark Soldier’s Tree from the same Owen yard — so course form alone may not separate them if the race is run at a strong gallop. I would not treat 8.4 as proof of superiority; my 9% win estimate reflects a narrow edge in a…

Off 9-6 from OR75 she avoids top-weight pressure, gets a high draw (41% of Thirsk 5f winners), and has clear going/distance suitability (G 2-6, D 3-15). Canaria Queen is next: light weight, draw 10, Tim Easterby in strong form, and a recent Thirsk second — but the drop from 6f and Class 5 company make her slightly less convincing as the win pick.

Forest Berry is the only runner with repeatable, trip-relevant form: second at Chepstow eight days ago over six furlongs on good to firm, beaten only 0.3L when strongly tried in the market, and she already carries the top official rating in the field (66). Prescott’s filly has the clearest fitness edge over Tiora Time, whose Epsom third was promising but over seven furlongs three weeks ago and a less direct guide to this trip, and over the debut brigade where Theft (draw 5, 75% rtf, Sioux Nation) and Pon Pon Dalia look the best bred but remain unproven. I rate her around 15% true win chance — well below the market’s implied ~18% on Tiora Time — which is why this stays a marginal play rather than a strong conviction bet.

The main danger is race shape from draw 12: if the early pace is strong through the middle stalls, she may need a perfect ride from James Sullivan to land a late challenge, while a well-handled low-draw debut such as Theft or Tiggywinkle could steal a soft lead. Cheeky Chesca is a possible Easterby pacemaker or inferior stable leg, but her recent seventh at Nottingham suggests she is not the main threat unless the setup collapses entirely.
